# Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign

**Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
**Status:** DRAFT ƒ?" NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Created:** 2026-10-10  
**Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review

## 1. Business objective

Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.

The exact intended flow is:

```text
historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  -> replay-local position lifecycle
  -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
```

Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
`signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.

The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.

## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence

The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:

1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
   use several shared Production services.
2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
   positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
   labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
   does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
   event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
   position was open.
4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
   `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
   Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
   were not retained.
5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
   have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
   path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
   live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
   isolation.

## 3. Boundaries

### In scope

- Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
- Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
- Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
- Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
- Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
- Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.

### Out of scope

- Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
- Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
- Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
- FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
- Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.

## 4. Contractual modes

| Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
| `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
| `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |

The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.

## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility

A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
state are written unless all of the following hold:

1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
   USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
   limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
   and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
   global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
   that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
   symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
   current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
   position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
   `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
   consumed range.
6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
   enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
   enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
   derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
   mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
   requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
   unavailable and the mode is refused.

`SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.

### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary

Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
`wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
position-management formulas.

The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.

## 6. Agreed behaviour changes

### 6.1 Replay starting state

Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.

The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
from Production while a run is executing.

### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority

Extract or complete pure shared policies for:

- initial and progressive sizing;
- reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
  maximum-open-position controls;
- buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
- position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
  state transitions;
- idempotency-key interpretation.

Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.

### 6.3 Price evidence

Replace the boolean/label derived from ƒ?oany event existsƒ?? with a coverage
result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
protection.

`BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.

### 6.4 Historical decision evidence

Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.

### 6.5 Event ordering and time

Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
single deterministic tie order matching Productionƒ?Ts committed sequence:

1. accepted price protection event;
2. resulting position/wallet state;
3. closed-candle analysis signal;
4. signal-driven position action.

If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.

### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour

The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.

| Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
| RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |

`ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.

## 7. Implementation change registry

No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.

| ID | Area | Required change |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-01 | run contract | Add persisted mode, evidence status, account profile/state ID, version/config fingerprint, and refusal reason to the replay run. |
| RPR-02 | replay account schema/service | Add `ReplayAccountProfile` and immutable run-start-state tables. Seed virtual state only from the selected Replay profile. |
| RPR-03 | wallet authority | Extract/reuse pure paper-wallet policy and provide Production/replay persistence adapters; remove direct Replay reads of `WalletSettingsRepository`. |
| RPR-03a | execution constraints | Split minimum-notional evaluation from live exchange constraint acquisition; inject immutable profile constraints into Replay and prohibit Replay Binance HTTP. |
| RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |
| RPR-05 | price evidence | Add interval coverage validator and replace the current any-event exact-price label. |
| RPR-06 | historical context | Add versioned persistence/lookup/qualification for order book and derivatives, or explicit parity refusal. |
| RPR-07 | timing/order | Persist/use deterministic committed event ordering and reject unknown ordering for parity runs. |
| RPR-08 | certificate | Add immutable parity certificate containing field comparisons, evidence counts, deviations, and final claim. |
| RPR-09 | removals | Execute only the approved RPR-RM entries, including migration/archive compatibility. |
| RPR-10 | UI/API | Make mode explicit; default to business-logic parity; display refusal/deviation/certificate details. |
| RPR-11 | tests | Add unit, integration, MySQL, complete scenario, no-look-ahead, and Production/Replay comparison tests below. |
| RPR-12 | docs | Update architecture, rules, changelog, investigation, operational runbook, and parity contract. |

## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions

- Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
- Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
  partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
- Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
  run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
- Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
- Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
  UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
- Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
  parity certificate.

## 9. Logging and observability registry

| ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
| RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
| RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
| RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
| RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
| RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |

## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact

- **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
  required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
  never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
  retired Replay-only output tables.
- **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
  and trading configuration remain unchanged.
- **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
  live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
  rule may change.
- **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
  or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
  isolated only as approved above.

## 11. Test and validation registry

| ID | Required proof |
|---|---|
| RPR-T-01 | The same profile inputs produce equal Production/replay sizing, reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, and global-position-limit decisions through shared policies. |
| RPR-T-01a | Replay refuses `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` when no immutable replay account profile is selected; it must never silently use a Production wallet row. |
| RPR-T-02 | A missing, unresolved, rejected, or coverage-gapped price event refuses business-logic parity and labels simulation correctly. |
| RPR-T-03 | Price-event/signal same-time ordering matches Production for TP, SL, profit lock, normal exit, and signal SELL. |
| RPR-T-04 | End-to-end initial BUY, progressive add, target extension, rebase, stop, profit lock, near-TP partial harvest, terminal exit, and P&L lifecycle match field-for-field. |
| RPR-T-05 | Historical order book/derivatives use only as-of persisted data; missing evidence refuses parity; no live HTTP occurs. |
| RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |
| RPR-T-06 | Multi-symbol global wallet/max-position test proves no isolated-symbol bypass. |
| RPR-T-07 | Cancellation/error tests prove Production tables remain unchanged and certificates cannot falsely pass. |
| RPR-T-08 | MySQL migration and transaction tests use a disposable database. |
| RPR-T-09 | Dataset and database replay inputs produce equivalent output for the same qualified evidence, or one path is removed. |
| RPR-T-10 | Removal tests prove baseline Replay cannot invoke FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, or pre-FIX-122 paths. |
| RPR-T-11 | Real historical parity suite compares selected known Production windows and produces stored certificates. |

## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance

- Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
  refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
- Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
  archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
- Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
  migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
- Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
  output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.

Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
authorization after both agents approve this same document.

===== FIX-154 DESIGN =====
# FIX-154 ƒ?" Trusted higher-timeframe BUY validation

Status: implementation design approved. FIX-154 is scheduled before the
remaining FIX-153 runtime release work.

## Purpose and boundary

FIX-154 replaces the current binary fresh-1h bearish veto with a trusted
higher-timeframe assessment. It does not relax the existing 5m confirmation
requirement, risk/reward result, current-1m BTC protection, or profile rules
except where a raw 1h decision would otherwise act as an untrusted veto.

The legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` is an unrelated shared-market
readiness design and is not an implementation source for this fix.

## Current integration points to replace

The 1h/5m blocks in all three paths below must be replaced as a whole by one
shared assessment call. Do not add this decision to `FinalDecisionService`.

1. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuy()` ƒ?" direct 1m BUYs.
2. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuyContext()` ƒ?" accumulated/setup
   authority, whose reference may be a 5m signal.
3. `OpportunityConsolidationService.evaluate()` ƒ?" consolidated BUYs.

Each replacement owns the current lookup, freshness, 5m veto, and 1h veto
block in that method. The remaining profile/consolidation logic consumes the
assessment's effective context; it must not reread the raw 1h decision as an
independent veto.

## Immutable signal evidence

Migration `V107` adds:

```sql
ALTER TABLE trade_signal ADD COLUMN data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL;
```

`AnalysisService` writes `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` through the
existing `TradeSignal.builder()` call for every newly generated signal on every
interval. Historic rows are never backfilled: `NULL` means unknown/invalid.

FIX-154 does not depend on `signal_mode` or any other FIX-153 runtime/schema
feature. `data_quality_valid = true` is sufficient evidence eligibility for
this release; false or `NULL` is unusable. Historical-only exclusion is added
later with FIX-153, after its own migration/runtime release is approved.

Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` is audit evidence only. It is never a
FIX-154 usability or hard-veto predicate. The current 1m BTC guard is unchanged.

## Time-bounded selection and fixed freshness

For a request with reference signal `R`, use `R.generatedAt` as `evaluatedAt`.
Select the latest same-symbol 5m and 1h signal such that both its generated
time and its candle close are at or before `evaluatedAt`; no wall-clock lookup
is permitted. Replay supplies the same historical reference time.

Existing fixed freshness limits remain authoritative:

| Interval | Maximum age |
|---|---:|
| 5m | 20 minutes |
| 1h | 3 hours |

`MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` and `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` retain their existing
meaning and priority when their respective interval is missing or stale. A
stale signal is not quietly converted into an allowed BUY just because it is
`NOT_TRUSTED` for FIX-154.

## Usability and 1h strength

A fresh row is usable for FIX-154 only if `data_quality_valid = true`.

FIX-154 v1 does not invent ADX/DMI/VWAP/CMF evidence, because those indicators
are not currently persisted. Its fixed strength definition is:

```text
HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE = 1h decision == STRONG_SELL
                         AND confidence_score >= 70
SOFT_CONFLICT          = fresh, quality-valid 1h decision == SELL
                         OR STRONG_SELL with confidence_score below 70
NOT_TRUSTED            = fresh but quality-invalid/unknown/historical-only 1h
                         OR fresh non-bearish 1h
```

`confidence_score` is the persisted effective confidence. `raw_confidence_score`
is explicitly not used. The constant `70` is a named FIX-154 configuration
default, persisted in the audit row, and may change only through a reviewed
strategy change with Replay regression.

## Exact 1h A- 5m decision matrix

First apply existing missing/stale checks. Only then apply this table.

| 1h state | 5m state | Final HTF result before profile | Profile inputs |
|---|---|---|---|
| missing/stale | any | existing `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` rejection | none |
| any | missing/stale | existing `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` rejection | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | `1H_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile, cap resulting first entry at 25% | effective 1h=`NEUTRAL`, raw 5m |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bearish, quality-valid | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO`; audit `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` | none |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bullish/neutral/watch, quality-valid | `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| fresh, quality-valid non-bearish 1h | non-bearish | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |

The profile call produces the final decision for every `continue to profile`
row. It may still return its existing insufficient-confirmation code. It must
not consult the raw 1h row after this mapping.

`5m quality=false/NULL` does not alter the legacy 5m fallback. A fresh raw
bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO`, exactly as today. It merely cannot
act as new FIX-154 hard-veto confirmation or a soft-conflict release.

The veto order is fixed: a bearish 5m returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any
trusted-1h result. If the 1h is hard, audit records `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT`
on that legacy outcome. When 5m is not bearish, a hard trusted 1h returns
`HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`. The only deliberate lasting relaxation is a trusted
SOFT bearish 1h: an otherwise allowed initial entry is capped at 25%.

## Profile contract

`conservativeBuy`, `balancedBuy`, `balancedBuyWithNeutralFiveException`, and
`aggressiveBuy` receive the assessment's effective 1h/5m contextƒ?"not raw
selected 1h evidence. A SOFT conflict maps to `NEUTRAL`, never `WATCH`, then
the validation result is capped with `min(profilePositionPercent, 25)`.
This means the neutral-5m/direct-BUY exception still requires a genuinely
bullish 1h and cannot be unlocked by SOFT evidence. An untrusted non-bearish
1h retains its raw decision so deployment does not accidentally tighten current
bullish authority.

The assessment result carries `maxInitialPercent`: `25` for `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
otherwise no cap. Every caller that selects a size must use
`min(itsCalculatedPercent, maxInitialPercent)` when the cap is present. This
includes direct validation, `validateBuyContext()` callers (the 5m-trigger
path and accumulated evidence), and
`OpportunityConsolidationService.consolidatedPositionPercent()`.

The raw selected signals remain audit evidence. Existing 5m requirements inside
profiles remain unchanged. HARD and untrusted-bearish outcomes never call a
profile method.

### Add-on contract

`ExecutionIntelligenceService.progressivePositionDecision()` already reads raw
1h evidence and requires `WATCH` or bullish support for `SCOUT_ENTRY`,
`CONFIRMATION_ADD`, and `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`. Keep that check unchanged and
outside the three replacement paths; it already blocks HARD, SOFT, and
untrusted-bearish 1h states. Do not route it through effective FIX-154 context,
which could widen behavior. When that existing check rejects while the assessed
band is SOFT, record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` as audit/metric evidence only.

## Append-only execution audit

FIX-154 is evaluated during execution/consolidation, not while a signal is
saved. Migration `V107` creates append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`:

- primary key ID;
- reference signal ID, evaluation path (`DIRECT_BUY`, `BUY_CONTEXT`,
  `CONSOLIDATION`, `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`, `CONFIRMATION_ADD`,
  `ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE_ADD`) and `evaluated_at`; no unique constraint;
- raw/effective 1h and 5m signal IDs, decisions, signal modes, candle closes,
  ages, data-quality values, and usability reasons;
- configured confidence threshold, band, legacy-veto result, profile context,
  final result/reason, Production/Replay mode, and creation timestamp.

The audit is written best-effort after the relevant execution/consolidation
decision is known. A duplicate or persistence failure is logged and increments
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`; it must never roll back, reject, or alter a
trading decision. It is evidence onlyƒ?"not a fence or an input to later trading.

## Deployment behavior

Immediately after deployment all pre-V107 1h/5m rows have
`data_quality_valid=NULL`. They are treated as `NOT_TRUSTED`; they cannot make a
new permissive decision: a bearish unknown 1h remains blocked, while a
non-bearish unknown 1h retains its existing profile authority. The health view
must expose `fix154.untrusted_bearish_1h_block_count` and
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`, `fix154.soft_capped_entry_count`, and
`fix154.soft_add_blocked_count`, grouped by execution path.

## Migration ordering and FIX-153 independence

The configured local database has V106 as its latest applied Flyway migration.
Therefore FIX-154 reserves `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql`.
Before this migration is applied anywhere, rename the currently unreleased
`V107__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql` to V108. Never rename or edit
an applied migration. Do not enable Flyway `outOfOrder`; deployment order is
made explicit instead.

## Required regression suite

- All three integration paths use the shared service and no longer contain raw
  `1H_BEARISH_VETO` branches.
- `STRONG_SELL` + `confidence_score >= 70` and bearish fresh valid 5m produces
  legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` with `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` audit; the same 1h
  with non-bearish 5m produces `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
- Strong/soft/untrusted 1h plus bullish, neutral, watch, bearish, missing,
  stale, false-quality, and NULL-quality 5m covers every matrix row and its
  final profile result.
- `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` (3h) and `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` (20m) retain their
  priority and codes.
- BNB #150a: persisted historical 1h bearish row with `NULL` quality =>
  `NOT_TRUSTED` and `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
- BNB #150b: Replay regenerates that same 1h with quality evidence and it is
  SOFT; no trusted hard veto is emitted and the final expected result is
  `REJECTED_RISK_REWARD` when the risk/reward guard fails.
- Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` does not change usability; current
  1m BTC behavior is unchanged.
- FIX-154 migration succeeds with no `signal_mode` column and its assessment
  has no FIX-153 schema/runtime dependency.
- No future signal/candle is selected in Production or Replay; both produce
  equivalent audit evidence for the same historical inputs.
- Pre-migration `NULL` quality is never treated as valid.
- Repeated execution/consolidation evaluations append audit rows and audit
  failure leaves the business decision intact.
- A SOFT initial entry never exceeds 25% through progressive, confirmation, or
  accumulated-evidence or consolidation sizing; existing progressive raw-1h
  support rules block add stages while the 1h remains bearish.

## Release conditions

Run Java 21 unit/integration tests, migration tests, Production/Replay parity,
and a bounded no-look-ahead historical replay before enabling FIX-154.
FIX-153 remains independent and retains its own release gates.

## Revision addendum ƒ?" Round 5 approval conditions

This addendum governs where it differs from earlier wording.

### Release build decision

The FIX-154 release build includes `V108__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql`
after the current unreleased FIX-153 V107 migration is renumbered to V108.
This is required even with reconciliation disabled: `candle_analysis_outcome`
is used by ordinary live signal processing, terminal worker outcomes, and the
closed-candle detector. Omitting V108 would cause missing-table SQL failures;
in particular, a normal signal-processing completion would roll back because
its outcome write is inside the processing transaction.

Including V108 ships FIX-153 reconciliation *schema only*. The reconciliation
runtime remains disabled and retains all of its separate release gates.

### No FIX-153 provenance dependency

Ignore earlier references to historical-only status or signal modes in the
FIX-154 band definition and audit payload. FIX-154 v1 eligibility depends only
on time-bounded selection and `data_quality_valid = true`; `signal_mode` is not
read, required, or stored by the FIX-154 audit.

### Effective context is transient

The assessment returns immutable effective decision values separately from the
loaded `TradeSignal` entities. No code may call `setDecision`, mutate, merge,
or save a selected 1h/5m signal to obtain effective `NEUTRAL` context. Profile
methods receive explicit effective decisions (or an immutable assessment
context), while the raw entity remains audit evidence only. A regression test
must load a SOFT 1h signal, execute the assessment/profile path, flush/clear,
and prove its stored decision is unchanged.

### Progressive-path evidence is observational

`progressivePositionDecision()` retains its existing raw-1h support check as
the only decision authority for scout/add paths. If that existing check passes,
FIX-154 performs no assessment lookup and writes no progressive audit row. If
it rejects because raw 1h is bearish, a best-effort audit-only assessment may
record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` when the band is SOFT. That observation must not
alter the rejection, allow an add, cause a database lookup on passing paths, or
propagate an audit failure into the trading decision.

V108 ships with FIX-154 because the current runtime writes
`candle_analysis_outcome`. Reconciliation remains disabled.

### Round 5 text clarification

This addendum removes any earlier contrary implication: FIX-154 v1 has no
`signal_mode` or historical-only predicate or audit field. Effective 1h/5m
decisions are separate immutable values and never mutate a loaded signal.
Progressive-path assessment is audit-only after its existing raw-1h check has
already rejected; it is skipped entirely when that check passes and never feeds
the scout/add decision.

## Implementation completion record ƒ?" 2026-10-10

The approved FIX-154 design is now implemented in the working tree.

- `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` creates the nullable signal
  quality evidence and append-only assessment audit table. No historical value
  is fabricated or backfilled.
- `AnalysisService` persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new
  signal.
- `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` owns the hard/soft/untrusted decision
  matrix. It returns immutable effective decisions and does not mutate a JPA
  signal.
- Direct BUY validation, accumulated-evidence authority, and opportunity
  consolidation invoke that one service. A SOFT result uses effective 1h
  `NEUTRAL` and applies the 25% initial-position cap. The legacy bearish-5m
  veto retains precedence. Progressive adds retain their existing raw 1h
  requirement and can only create best-effort audit evidence after rejection.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore` is append-only and runs in an independent
  transaction. It catches insert failures so audit availability cannot alter a
  business decision.

Focused Java 21 verification passed:
`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`,
`TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`, and
`OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`.

This is implementation completion, not production deployment approval. Apply
V107/V108 only through the normal clean Java 21 release pipeline and complete
the required migration, Replay-parity, and broader regression validation before
enabling it in Production.


===== HANDOFF =====
# FIX-154 ƒ?" Implementation Handoff for Code Review

Date: 2026-10-10  
Status: implemented in the local working tree; not deployed.

## Scope

FIX-154 changes only higher-timeframe BUY authority. It does not enable or
change FIX-153 reconciliation/runtime behavior.

The objective is to distinguish trusted, strong bearish 1h evidence from
unknown or weaker evidence without changing a stored historical signal.

## Implemented policy

1. The current 1m BUY still requires fresh 5m and 1h context.
2. A bearish 5m returns the existing `5M_BEARISH_VETO` first.
3. A bearish 1h with `data_quality_valid` `NULL` or `false` returns
   `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
4. A trusted 1h `STRONG_SELL` with `confidence_score >= 70` returns
   `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
5. A trusted 1h `SELL`, or `STRONG_SELL` with confidence below 70, is a
   SOFT conflict. It passes effective 1h `NEUTRAL` to profile logic and caps
   every allowed initial entry at 25%.
6. A non-bearish 1h with unknown quality keeps its raw decision; FIX-154 does
   not tighten bullish/non-bearish authority merely because historical quality
   is absent.
7. Existing progressive/add logic retains its raw 1h requirement. A SOFT
   conflict cannot authorize an add. Optional `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence
   is observational only.

## Database changes

`V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` adds:

- `trade_signal.data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL`.
- Replay parity columns on `trade_signal_test`:
  `btc_context_candle_close_time`, `btc_context_age_seconds`,
  `btc_context_freshness_threshold_seconds`, `signal_mode`, and
  `data_quality_valid`.
- Append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, including effective/raw decisions,
  candle-close-based age, quality evidence, the hard-confirmation marker,
  cap, legacy veto and final result.

Historical `data_quality_valid` stays `NULL`; no value is backfilled.

## Included production code

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `domain/TradeSignal.java` | Maps nullable `data_quality_valid`. |
| `service/AnalysisService.java` | Persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` at signal generation. |
| `service/HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.java` | Pure FIX-154 decision matrix; returns detached effective decisions and never mutates entities. |
| `service/TradeExecutionValidationService.java` | Applies assessment to direct BUY and contextual/accumulated authority; enforces SOFT cap; records non-blocking audit evidence. |
| `service/OpportunityConsolidationService.java` | Applies the same matrix and cap to consolidation. |
| `execution/service/ExecutionIntelligenceService.java` | Keeps existing progressive raw-1h guard; invokes optional audit-only SOFT evidence after its rejection. Uses `SETUP_WAKEUP` audit path for the 5m wake-up caller. |
| `service/BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore.java` | Snapshots and asynchronously persists best-effort Production audit rows. Replay writes no Production audit row. |
| `config/Fix154AuditAsyncConfig.java` | Dedicated one-thread, bounded executor; audit saturation drops evidence rather than borrowing a trading connection. |
| `service/Fix154Metrics.java` | Non-gating counters for untrusted 1h blocks, audit failures, SOFT caps and SOFT add blocks. |
| `service/Fix154DiagnosticsController.java` | Read-only `GET /api/fix154/health` metrics endpoint. |
| `service/Fix154RuntimeSchemaChecks.java` | Checks V107-required production and Replay schema before runtime admission. |

## Replay behavior

The regular reflection-based Replay writer persists every JPA `TradeSignal`
column. V107 therefore updates `trade_signal_test` alongside production.
FIX-154 audit rows are deliberately skipped while `ExecutionReplayScope` is
active because replay-generated entities do not have Production IDs and parity
is recorded by Replayƒ?Ts own evidence tables.

## Tests included

- `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`
  - hard, soft, untrusted, 5m-veto precedence, 69/70 boundary, BNB #150a and
    #150b policy cases, and unknown non-bearish authority.
- `TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`
  - direct/context SOFT caps, conservative behavior, neutral-5m exception is
    not unlocked, audit failure isolation, Production/Replay parity, and
    future-context exclusion.
- `OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`
  - SOFT cap through consolidation.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStoreTest`
  - Replay does not write a Production audit row.
- `Fix154MigrationContractTest`
  - V107 contains production, Replay and audit schema contracts.

## Verification performed

Executed successfully with Java 21:

```text
mvn -q -Dtest=HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest,
TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest,
OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest,
BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStoreTest,
Fix154MigrationContractTest test
```

## Reviewer focus / remaining external proof

All FIX-154 implementation code described above is present in this local
workspace. The remaining release proof is execution, not an identified code
gap:

1. Run Flyway V107 against a dedicated disposable MySQL schema and verify
   `trade_signal`, `trade_signal_test`, and `buy_htf_assessment_audit`.
2. Run the full application/replay regression suite against that schema before
   Production deployment.
3. Confirm `flyway_schema_history` on the target database has not already
   applied V107; once applied, any correction must use a new forward migration.

No production database, service, or deployment configuration was changed by

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (1-285) =====
1:# Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture
2:
3:Status labels used in this document:
4:
5:- **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
6:- **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
7:- **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
8:  while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
9:
10:## Current release status
11:
12:**FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
13:It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
14:in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
15:two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
16:
17:FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
18:and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
19:
20:## System purpose
21:
22:The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
23:market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
24:and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
25:Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
26:
27:## Runtime and data ownership
28:
29:| Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
30:|---|---|---|
31:| Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
32:| Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
33:| Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
34:| Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
35:| Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
36:
37:## Current primary flow
38:
39:```text
40:Binance WebSocket kline
41:  -> BinanceKlineService
42:  -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
43:      -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
44:      -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
45:      -> observer transaction
46:      -> close-dispatch transaction
47:  -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
48:  -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
49:  -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
50:  -> TechnicalIndicatorService
51:  -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
52:  -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
53:  -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
54:```
55:
56:`KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
57:close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
58:run on the Binance ingestion thread.
59:
60:## Current core persistence
61:
62:| Table/domain | Purpose |
63:|---|---|
64:| `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
65:| `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
66:| `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
67:| `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
68:| `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
69:| Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
70:
71:## Legacy/shared-market path
72:
73:**Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
74:
75:The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
76:`SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
77:tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
78:and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
79:dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
80:
81:The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
82:and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
83:trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
84:
85:`V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
86:that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
87:bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
88:not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
89:
90:## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
91:
92:The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
93:ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
94:path must never run together.
95:
96:Implemented safeguards:
97:
98:- Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
99:  closed candle.
100:- A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
101:  result plus in-process admission coordination.
102:- A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
103:  the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
104:- Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
105:  their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
106:- Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
107:  pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
108:- Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
109:- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
110:  query retired shared-market tables.
111:- Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
112:  runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
113:  provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
114:  automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
115:  candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
116:  the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
117:- Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
118:  boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
119:  not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
120:  analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
121:  repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
122:  gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
123:  provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
124:  and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
125:  case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
126:  not proof that the gap is closed.
127:- Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
128:  duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
129:  REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
130:  terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
131:  a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
132:  failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
133:- The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
134:  not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
135:  queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
136:  protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
137:- Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
138:  V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
139:  pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
140:  request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
141:  unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
142:- Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
143:  protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
144:- Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
145:  database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
146:  separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
147:  HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
148:  General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
149:  clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
150:  Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
151:  transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
152:  REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
153:- An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
154:  quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
155:  the controlled drain window.
156:
157:## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
158:
159:For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
160:
161:```text
162:PENDING
163:  -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
164:  -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
165:  -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
166:```
167:
168:Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
169:Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
170:direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
171:
172:The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
173:protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
174:Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
175:`REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
176:live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
177:
178:The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
179:operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
180:
181:## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
182:
183:FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
184:
185:Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
186:only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
187:strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
188:evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
189:remains unchanged.
190:
191:The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
192:`HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
193:a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
194:rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
195:
196:The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
197:It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
198:the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
199:
200:## Deployment posture
201:
202:Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
203:implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
204:
205:Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
206:real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
207:tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
208:runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
209:two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
210:enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
211:compile the Java 21 project.
212:
213:No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
214:without explicit user approval.
215:
216:## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
217:
218:The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
219:core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
220:outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
221:boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
222:that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
223:
224:The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
225:but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
226:run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
227:can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
228:barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
229:atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
230:and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
231:must stay `false`.
232:
233:The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
234:contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
235:mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
236:starting.
237:
238:### Shared Binance REST admission
239:
240:`BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
241:immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
242:response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
243:the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
244:calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
245:of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
246:
247:### Outcome-schema startup invariant
248:
249:The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
250:terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
251:though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
252:runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
253:table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
254:when they attempt their first outcome write.
255:# FIX-154 implementation update Г?" 2026-10-10
256:
257:`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
258:the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
259:entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
260:ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
261:for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
262:uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
263:initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
264:the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
265:
266:`buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
267:best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
268:execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
269:be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
270:existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
271:that guard has already rejected an add.
272:
273:`GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
274:blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
275:
276:## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation Г?" enabled 2026-10-10
277:
278:In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
279:five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
280:missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
281:the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
282:An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
283:plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
284:dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
285:ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

1:# Local candle coverage check Г?" 2026-10-09
2:
3:- Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
4:- Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.
5:- Continuity: 5m and 1h had no internal gaps in the inspected window. Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
6:- Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.
7:
8:# Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09
9:
10:- System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
11:- The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
12:- Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
13:- `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
14:- 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
15:- No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.
16:

20:- Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
21:- Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.

23:# ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09
24:
25:- Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
26:- Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
27:- Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
28:- Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
29:- Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
30:- Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.
31:- Assessment: the trade followed the implemented rules once local analysis resumed, and the trade was entered/exited promptly. It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54Г?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate. Whether the unobserved earlier period would have produced an executable BUY is uncertain without a controlled Replay run; none was started because that would create replay state.

86:# SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
87:
88:- Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
89:  This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
90:- Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
91:  continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
92:- Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
93:  evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
94:  to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
95:- Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
96:  evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
97:  hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
98:  1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
99:  target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
100:  drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
101:  exit eligibility.
102:- Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
103:  or near-TP distance alone.
104:- Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
105:  with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
106:  1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
107:  protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
108:  lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
109:- Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
110:  paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
111:  comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
112:  gross profit is a net gain.
113:- Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
114:  unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
115:  near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
116:  calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
117:  pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
118:  qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
119:  a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
120:  momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
121:  and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
122:  false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
123:  reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
124:- Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
125:  entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
126:  distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
127:  evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
128:  intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
129:  floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
130:  that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
131:  improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
132:  viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
133:- Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
134:  85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
135:  quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
136:  current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
137:  unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
138:  This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
139:  Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
140:  B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
141:  must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
142:  tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
143:  use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
144:- Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
145:  from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
146:  high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
147:  `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
148:  breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
149:  fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
150:  numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
151:  length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
152:  before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
153:  out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
154:- Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
155:  before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
156:  Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
157:  order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
158:  conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
159:  with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
160:  `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
161:  breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
162:  breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
163:  avoided by A.
164:
165:# Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10
166:
167:- Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
168:  the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
169:  database data, or Replay result was changed.
170:- Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
171:  `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
172:  through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
173:  `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
174:  profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
175:  classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
176:  consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
177:  visible, matching the documented live ordering.
178:- Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
179:  identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
180:  manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
181:  position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
182:  reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
183:  `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
184:  real execution latency.
185:- Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
186:  persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
187:  context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
188:  stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
189:  whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
190:  are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
191:  required historical evidence exists and is reported.
192:- Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
193:  `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
194:  does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
195:  protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
196:  are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
197:  should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
198:- Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
199:  parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
200:  Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
201:  Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
202:  price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
203:- Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
204:  and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
205:  evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
206:  to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
207:  scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
208:  release-equivalence certificate.
===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md (1-21) =====
1:# FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10
2:
3:- `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
4:  treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
5:- A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
6:- A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
7:  profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
8:  capped at 25%.
9:- A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
10:- Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
11:  never authorizes an add.
12:
13:# FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10
14:
15:- Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
16:  the data-quality gate.
17:- Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
18:  Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
19:  analysis, a signal, or a trade.
20:- A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
21:  generation after a repaired quality window.
